C^{\bullet}_{\aug}(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}_{U})=\underset{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s>1}{\colim}{}\hskip 2.84544ptC^{\bullet}_{\aug}(\mathcal{Y}_{s},\mathcal{F}).

The result follows, because cohomology commutes with filtered colimits.
∎

Theorem 4.1.8
.

We have
\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U}}=\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}_{U}
for every abelian sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on an affinoid variety
X
and every rational subdomain
U
of
X
.

Proof.

For every open subset
V
of
C:=\overline{\tilde{U}}
, let

(i_{\pre}^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})(V)=\colim\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(W)

where the colimit runs over all open subsets
W
of
\tilde{X}
containing
i(V)=V
. This defines a presheaf
i_{\pre}^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
on
C
, and
i^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
is the sheafification of this presheaf. We will first show that

(26)

(i^{-1}_{\pre}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})(\tilde{Z}\cap C)=\underset{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s>1}{\colim}{}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{F}(Z\cap U(s))

for every affinoid subdomain
Z
of
X
. Consider the open subsets
W
of
\tilde{X}
containing
\tilde{Z}\cap C
. Clearly those
W
already contained in
\tilde{Z}
are cofinal; since
\tilde{Z}\cap C
is the closure of
\tilde{Z}\cap\tilde{U}=\widetilde{Z\cap U}
in
\tilde{Z}
, Lemma
4.1.6
tells us that the subsets of the form
(Z\cap U)(s)^{\tilde{}}
are cofinal amongst these. Now
(\ref{ipreForm})
follows since
(Z\cap U)(s)=Z\cap U(s)
for each
s
.

Because the functor
Z\mapsto\colim\limits_{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s>1}\mathcal{F}(Z\cap U(s))
satisfies the sheaf condition with respect to finite coverings of
Z
by affinoid subdomains of
Z
by Lemma
4.1.7
, and because the
\tilde{Z}\cap C
form a basis for the topology of
C
,
(\ref{ipreForm})
now implies that the values of
i^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
agree with those of
i^{-1}_{\pre}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
on these basic open sets.

Hence we can calculate that

\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U}}(Y)=(i_{\ast}i^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})(\tilde{Y})=(i^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})(\tilde{Y}\cap C)=\underset{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s>1}{\colim}{}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{F}(Y\cap U(s))

for every affinoid subdomain
Y
of
X
as claimed.
∎

Theorem
4.1.8
implies, in particular, that the sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}_{U}
in fact does
not
depend on the choice of presentation of
U
as a rational subdomain of
X
. We also see that we should think of
\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U}}
as being the sheaf of
sections of
\mathcal{F}
overconvergent into the complement of
U
.

Corollary 4.1.9
.

Let
U
be a rational subdomain of the affinoid variety
X
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. Then the higher Čech cohomology of
\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U}}
is zero.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
4.1.8
, Lemma
4.1.7
and Tate’s Acyclicity Theorem
[
22
, Theorem 4.5.2 and Theorem 4.2.2]
.
∎

4.2.
The
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
-module
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}

In this section we study a Kummer-type finite étale covering of the rigid-analytic affine line minus finitely many rational points. We recall that any
K
-scheme
X
of locally finite type has an analytification
X^{\an}
that is a rigid analytic space over
K
. The construction
X\mapsto X^{\an}
is functorial in
X
, and we refer the reader to
[
35
, §1.6]
or
[
16
, §5.4]
for more details.

We fix
\mathcal{S}=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{h}\}
, a finite subset of
K
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Given
u\in K(x)
a rational function with factorisation in
\overline{K}(x)

u=\lambda\prod_{i=1}^{n}(x-b_{i})^{k_{i}}\in K(x)

for some
\lambda\in K^{\times}
, pairwise distinct
b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\in\overline{K}
and non-zero integers
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}
. The
set of finite zeroes/poles
of
u
is

\mathcal{S}(u):=\{b_{i}\mid i=1,\ldots n\}.

We write
v_{b_{i}}(u)=k_{i}\mbox{ for }i=1,\ldots n\mbox{ and }v_{b}(u)=0\mbox{ for }b\in\overline{K}\backslash\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\}
.

We fix
u
such that
\mathcal{S}(u)
is a subset of
\mathcal{S}
.
Thus

u=\lambda\prod_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(x-a)^{v_{a}(u)}

with the
v_{a}(u)
integers that may be zero and some
\lambda\in K^{\times}
. We consider the open embedding of rigid
K
-analytic spaces

j:=\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}:=(\Spec K\left[x\right])^{\an},

and we introduce the finite étale covering

f:Z(u,d)\to\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}

where

(27)

Z(u,d):=\left(\Spec K\left[x,\frac{1}{x-a_{1}},\cdots,\frac{1}{x-a_{h}}\right][z]/(z^{d}-u)\right)^{\an}.

Note that
f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{Z(u,d)}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}}
-module of rank
d
:

f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{Z(u,d)}=\bigoplus_{m=0}^{d-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}}z^{m}.

Since
f:Z(u,d)\to\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}
is étale, the discussion in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{DivTwDer}
shows that
f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{Z(u,d)}
is in fact a
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}}
-module, and that this is a decomposition of
f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{Z(u,d)}
into a direct sum of
d
line bundles with flat connection on
\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}
. Applying (
10
) with
u
replaced by
u^{-m}
shows that the
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}}
-action on each summand in the above decomposition is given by

(28)

\partial_{x}\cdot z^{m}=\frac{m}{d}\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{v_{a}(u)}{x-a}\right)\hskip 2.84544ptz^{m}.

Our main goal in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{KummerSect}
is to study the following
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{A}}
-module.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Define
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d):=j_{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}}z)
.

The other line bundles with connection
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}}z^{m}
can be dealt with by replacing
u
by
u^{m}
so no generality is lost by restricting to the case
m=1
.

Definition 4.2.3
.

For each
t\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
we define

U(\mathcal{S})_{t}:=U_{t}:=\{z\in\mathbb{A}:|z-a|\geqslant t\quad\mbox{for all}\quad a\in\mathcal{S}\}.

Clearly
U_{t}
is a subset of
\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}
, we have
U_{t}\subseteq U_{t^{\prime}}
whenever
t^{\prime}<t
, and

\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}=\bigcup\limits_{t>0}U_{t}.

We will study the
\mathcal{D}
-module
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)
through its approximations
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U}_{t}}
, as
t
shrinks to zero: one can use Proposition
4.1.3
to see that

\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)=\lim\limits_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\longleftarrow}}{{t>0}}}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U}_{t}}.

We will give a proof of another form of this statement in Lemma
4.3.3
.

We assume until the end of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{KummerSect}
that
t\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
.

Next let us see what the local sections of these objects
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U}_{t}}
look like.

Lemma 4.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an abelian sheaf on
\mathbb{A}
. Then

(\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U_{t}}})(X)=\underset{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s<t}{\colim}{}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{F}(X\cap U_{s}).

for every affinoid subdomain
X
of
\mathbb{A}
.

Proof.

It is easy to verify by looking at the
\bf{C}
-points that

X\cap U_{t}=X\left(\frac{t}{x-a_{1}},\ldots\frac{t}{x-a_{h}}\right).

Recall now the wide open neighbourhoods
(X\cap U_{t})(r)
of the affinoid subdomain
X\cap U_{t}
of
X
from Definition
4.1.5
. We can now apply Lemma
4.1.2
together with Theorem
4.1.8
to compute

(\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U_{t}}})(X)=(\mathcal{F}_{|X})_{\overline{X\cap U_{t}}}(X)=\underset{r\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},r>1}{\colim}{}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{F}((X\cap U_{t})(r)).

But for any
r\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
with
r>1
, we have

(X\cap U_{t})(r)(\mathbf{C})=\bigcap\limits_{i=1}^{h}\{y\in X(\mathbf{C}):t\leqslant r|(y-a_{i})|\}=X\cap U_{t/r},

so
(\mathcal{F}_{\overline{U_{t}}})(X)=\underset{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s<t}{\colim}{}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{F}(X\cap U_{s})
as required.∎

Corollary 4.2.5
.

We have
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}})_{\overline{U_{t}}}\hskip 2.84544ptz
as sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}}
-modules.

Proof.

Let
X
be an affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{A}
and let
s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
. Since
X\cap U_{s}\subset\mathbb{A}\backslash\mathcal{S}
, we see that
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)(X\cap U_{s})=\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})z
. So by Lemma
4.2.4
,

(29)

\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)=\underset{s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|},s<t}{\colim}{\hskip 2.84544pt}\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})\hskip 2.84544ptz.\qed

Recall from Definition
2.3.9
(a) that
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
is the
G
-topology on
\mathbb{A}
which consists of those affinoid subdomains such that
r(X)\leqslant\varpi/t
. In view of Corollary
2.3.17
, we see that
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
if and only if
\rho(X)\geqslant t
. The following technical Lemma is necessary to prove Lemma
4.2.7
and Proposition
4.2.9
.

Lemma 4.2.6
.

Let
X
be a
(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
-admissible affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{A}
, and let
s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
be such that
s<t
. Then
r(X\cap U_{s})\leqslant\varpi/s
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
2.3.13
, we can replace
K
by some finite field extension and thereby assume that
s\in K^{\times}
. Choose a disc
\mathbb{D}:=\Sp K\langle x/s_{0}\rangle
with
s_{0}\in K^{\times}
large enough to contain
X
and note that
\mathbb{D}\cap U_{s}=C(\alpha,\mathbf{s})
where
s_{1}:=s_{2}:=\cdots:=s_{h}:=s
,
\alpha_{0}=0
and
\alpha_{i}:=a_{i}
for each
i=1,\ldots,h
. Then
X\cap U_{s}=X\cap C(\alpha,\mathbf{s})
and
r(C(\alpha,\mathbf{s}))=\frac{\varpi}{\rho(C(\alpha,\mathbf{s}))}=\frac{\varpi}{s}
by Corollary
2.3.17
, so

r(X\cap U_{s})=r(X\cap C(\alpha,\mathbf{s}))\leqslant\max\{r(X),r(C(\alpha,\mathbf{s}))\}=\max\{r(X),\varpi/s\}

by Proposition
2.3.2
. Our assumptions on
X
,
s
and
t
imply that
r(X)\leqslant\varpi/t<\varpi/s
, so
\max\{r(X),r(C(\alpha,\mathbf{s}))\}=\varpi/s
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.7
.

The
\mathcal{D}
-action on the restriction of
(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}})_{\overline{U_{t}}}
to
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
extends to
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
.

Proof.

Let
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
; we must show that the
\mathcal{D}(X)
-action on
(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}})_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)
extends to an action of
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)
and
Q\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
. Then by Lemma
4.2.4
and Definition
2.3.9
(d) we can find some
s^{\prime}<t
and
r>\varpi/t
such that
f\in\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s^{\prime}})
and
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{r}(X)
. Choose
s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
such that

\max\{\varpi/r,s^{\prime}\}<s<t.

Then
f\in\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})
and
r>\varpi/s
, so
Q\cdot f\in\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})\subset\mathcal{O}_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)
by Lemma
4.2.6
and Lemma
2.3.12
(a). Thus the
\mathcal{D}(X)
-action on
(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}})_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)
extends to an action of
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
as required.
∎

We assume for the remainder of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{KummerSect}
that
p\nmid d
.

Lemma 4.2.8
.

Let
r>0
and let
Y
be a
\partial_{x}/r
-admissible affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{A}-\mathcal{S}
. Then the natural
\mathcal{D}(Y)
-action on
\mathcal{O}(Y)z
extends to a
\mathcal{D}_{r}(Y)
-action.

Proof.

Since
\partial_{x}(z)=\frac{1}{d}\frac{\partial_{x}(u)}{u}z
, the
\mathcal{D}(Y)
-action on
\mathcal{O}(Y)z
is given by

(P,fz)\mapsto\sigma_{r}(\theta_{u^{-1},d}(P))(f)z,

where
\theta_{u^{-1},d}
was defined in Lemma
2.4.1
and
\sigma_{r}
was defined in Lemma
2.3.12
. Thus to extend it to a
\mathcal{D}_{r}(Y)
-action it suffices to extend the automorphism
\theta_{u^{-1},d}
of
\mathcal{D}(Y)
to an automorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{r}(Y)
; that this can be done is guaranteed by Theorem
2.4.9
.
∎

This result enables us to extend Lemma
4.2.7
in the following manner.

Proposition 4.2.9
.

The
\mathcal{D}
-action on the restriction of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
to
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
extends to
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
.

Proof.

Let
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
and suppose that
f\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)
and
Q\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
. Then by (
29
) and Definition
2.3.9
(d) we can find some
s^{\prime}<t
and
r>\varpi/t
such that
f\in\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s^{\prime}})z
and
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{r}(X)
.

Choose
s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
such that

\max\{\varpi/r,s^{\prime}\}<s<t.

Then
f\in\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})z
so if the
\mathcal{D}(X)
-action on
\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})z
extends to
\mathcal{D}_{r}(X)
then
Q\cdot f\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)
.

Now
r(X\cap U_{s})\leqslant\varpi/s
by Lemma
4.2.6
and
\varpi/s<r
, so
X\cap U_{s}
is
\partial_{x}/r
-admissible. Hence the
\mathcal{D}(X\cap U_{s})
-action on
\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})z
extends to
\mathcal{D}_{r}(X\cap U_{s})
by Lemma
4.2.8
. Since
X
and
X\cap U_{s}
are both
\partial_{x}/r
-admissible, by Proposition
2.3.6
(b) there is a ring homomorphism
\mathcal{D}_{r}(X)\to\mathcal{D}_{r}(X\cap U_{s})
extending
\mathcal{D}(X)\to\mathcal{D}(X\cap U_{s})
, and we deduce that the
\mathcal{D}(X)
-action on
\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})z
extends to
\mathcal{D}_{r}(X)
as required.
∎

In the remainder of this section we will establish an explicit presentation of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
as a
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
-module, assuming
t\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
is sufficiently small and a positivity condition on the exponents of
u
.

Definition 4.2.10
.

We define

(1)

\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}:=\prod\limits_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(x-a)
, and

(2)

R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d):=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}\partial_{x}-\frac{1}{d}\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{S}}v_{a}(u)\prod\limits_{b\in\mathcal{S}\backslash\{a\}}(x-b)=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}(\partial_{x}-\frac{1}{d}\dlog(u))
.

Note that
R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A})
. We will write
R(u,d)=R_{\mathcal{S}(u)}(u,d)
and
\Delta=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}(u)}
.

Lemma 4.2.11
.

There is a complex of
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
-modules on
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}

0\to\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot z}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}\to 0.

Proof.

Note that the restriction of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
to
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
is a
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
-module by Proposition
4.2.9
. The second non-zero arrow sends
Q\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
to
Q\cdot z\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
. By definition,
R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}\partial_{x}-\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{h}v_{a_{i}}(u)\prod_{j\neq i}(x-a_{j})\in\mathcal{D}
, so
R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)\in\mathcal{D}_{\varpi/t}^{\dagger}
as well. A direct calculation shows that
R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)\cdot z=0
.
∎

We will now carry out three local calculations that will be used to prove that the complex appearing in Lemma
4.2.11
is exact, at least when the
a_{i}
live in different holes of
U_{t}
, and thus provides a finite presentation for
\mathcal{M}(u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
as a
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
-module. The first shows that the restriction of the complex to
U_{t}
is exact.

Proposition 4.2.12
.

Let
Y
be an
(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
-admissible affinoid subdomain of
U_{t}
. Then the following complex is exact:

0\to\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot z}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)\to 0.

Proof.

Since
Y
is contained in
U_{t}
,
u
and
\Delta
are both units in
\mathcal{O}(Y)
and it follows from Lemma
4.1.2
that
\mathcal{O}(Y)z\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)
is an isomorphism. By Corollary
2.4.11
,
Q\mapsto z\hskip 2.84544ptQ\hskip 2.84544ptz^{-1}
induces an automorphism
\theta_{u,d}
of
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)
, then by Definition
4.2.10
(b) and Lemma
2.4.1

\theta_{u,d}^{-1}(R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d))=\Delta\partial_{x}.

Since
\Delta
is a unit in
\mathcal{O}(Y)
, we see that
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\cdot\theta_{u,d}(\partial_{x})
. By construction of the rings
\mathcal{D}_{r}(Y)
, there is a direct sum decomposition of
\mathcal{O}(Y)
-modules:
\mathcal{D}_{r}(Y)=\mathcal{O}(Y)\oplus\mathcal{D}_{r}(Y)\cdot\partial_{x}
whenever
r>\varpi/t
. Therefore we also have the decomposition

\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)=\mathcal{O}(Y)\oplus\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\theta_{u,d}(\partial_{x})

and the result now follows easily.
∎

We record an easy but important consequence of the proof of Proposition
4.2.12
.

Corollary 4.2.13
.

Let
Y
be a
(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
-admissible subdomain of
U_{t}
. Then the
\mathcal{O}(Y)
-module
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)
is free of rank
1
, and it is simple as a
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)
-module whenever
Y
is connected as an affinoid domain.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the proof of Proposition
4.2.12
.

For the second statement it suffices to show that
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)
is a simple
\mathcal{D}(Y)
-module via restriction whenever
Y
is connected. Given the first part this follows from
[
8
, Lemma 4.3.2]
, because
\mathcal{T}(Y)
is freely generated by
\partial_{x}
as a
\mathcal{O}(Y)
-module.
∎

Our next step is to prove that the complex in Lemma
4.2.11
is exact in the case when
|\mathcal{S}|=1
under appropriate mild conditions on
u
. First we need a couple of technical results to enable us to a make a certain estimate. Together, they amount to a slightly more general version of
[
12
, Lemme 4.2.1]
.

Lemma 4.2.14
.

Suppose that
V
is a a reduced
K
-affinoid variety and
f\in\mathcal{O}(V)
. Then there is
s\in\sqrt{|K|^{\times}}
with
s<t
such that whenever
g\in\mathcal{O}(V)
vanishes on
V(t/f)
,
g
also vanishes on
V(s/f)
.

Proof.

We first consider the case where
V
is irreducible so that
\mathcal{O}(V)
is an integral domain. Suppose first that
V(t/f)
is non-empty. Then the restriction map
\mathcal{O}(V)\to\mathcal{O}(V(t/f))
is injective by the proof of
[
10
, Proposition 4.2]
, so any choice of
s
will do. On the other hand, if
V(t/f)=\emptyset
, then
|f|_{V}<t
by the maximum modulus principle, so choosing
|f|_{V}<s<t
ensures that
V(s/f)=\emptyset
and yields the result.

In the general case, let
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}
be the irreducible components of
V
and let
f_{i}:=f_{|V_{i}}
be the restriction of
f
to
V_{i}
for each
i=1,\cdots,n
. Now, for each
s<t
with
s\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
there is a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.94739pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.80557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.36494pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 56.36494pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(V(s/f))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 158.8598pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.50037pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 158.8598pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(V(t/f))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 187.45703pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-22.94739pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}(V_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.94739pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.94739pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}(V_{i}(s/f_{i}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 148.05334pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 148.05334pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}(V_{i}(t/f_{i})).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The vertical arrow on the left is injective because
V
is reduced. Hence the middle vertical arrow is injective, because the restriction map
\mathcal{O}(V)\to\mathcal{O}(V(s/f))
is flat.

Since each
V_{i}
is irreducible, we may find
s_{i}<t
in
\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
such that every
g\in\mathcal{O}(V_{i})
vanishing on
V_{i}(t/f_{i})
also vanishes on
V_{i}(s_{i}/f_{i})
. By enlarging each
s_{i}
if necessary, we may assume that
s_{1}=\cdots=s_{n}=:s
, say.

Suppose now that
g\in\mathcal{O}(V)
vanishes on
V(t/f)
. Then by the commutativity of the diagram,
g_{|V_{i}}
vanishes on
V_{i}(t/f_{i})
for all
i
. Hence by the above paragraph,
g_{|V_{i}}
also vanishes on
V_{i}(s/f_{i})
for all
i
. Using the fact that the middle arrow in the diagram is injective, we deduce that
g
also vanishes on
V(s/f)
as required.
∎

Lemma 4.2.15
.

Let
V
be a reduced
K
-affinoid variety and
f\in\mathcal{O}(V)
. Suppose moreover that
(v_{n})
is a sequence in
\mathcal{O}(V)
such that

(1)

\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}v_{n}T^{n}\in\mathcal{O}(V)[[T]]
has radius of convergence greater than
1/t
, and

(2)

the series
\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}v_{n}f^{-n}
converges to zero in
\mathcal{O}(V(t/f))
.

Then, writing
w_{m}:=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{m}v_{n}f^{m-n}\in\mathcal{O}(V)
for each
m\geqslant 0
, the radius of convergence of
\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}w_{m}T^{m}\in\mathcal{O}(V)[[T]]
is also greater than
1/t
.

Proof.

By condition (i) we may choose
r\in\sqrt{|K|^{\times}}
such that
r<t
and

\lim_{n\to\infty}|v_{n}|_{V}/r^{n}=0.

Thus we may define
L:=\sup_{n\geqslant 0}|v_{n}|_{V}/r^{n}<\infty
and
W:=V(r/f)
. Because
|f^{-1}|_{W}\leqslant 1/r
, we have
|v_{n}f^{-n}|_{W}\leqslant|v_{n}|_{V}/r^{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, so the series
\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}v_{n}f^{-n}
converges in
\mathcal{O}(W)
to an element
w
, say. Note that condition (ii) implies that
w
vanishes on
W(t/f)
.

Using Lemma
4.2.14
we may choose
s\in\sqrt{|K|^{\times}}
with
s<t
such that whenever
g\in\mathcal{O}(W)
vanishes on
W(t/f)
,
g
also vanishes on
W(s/f)
. By enlarging
s
if necessary, we may also assume that
r<s
. Thus,
w_{|W(s/f)}=0
.

We cover
V
by its two affinoid subdomains
V(f/s)
and
V(s/f)
. Noting that
w_{|V(s/f)}=0
because
V(s/f)=W(s/f)
, we can estimate
|w_{m}|_{V(s/f)}
as follows:

|w_{m}|_{V(s/f)}=\left|-\sum_{n>m}v_{n}f^{m-n}\right|_{V(s/f)}\leqslant\sup_{n>m}Lr^{-n}s^{m-n}\leqslant Ls^{m}.

To estimate
|w_{m}|_{V(f/s)}
, we note that
|v_{n}|_{V(f/s)}\leqslant|v_{n}|_{V}\leqslant Lr^{n}
for all
n\geqslant 0
, so

|w_{m}|_{V(f/s)}\leqslant\max_{0\leqslant n\leqslant m}|v_{n}f^{m-n}|_{V_{1}}\leqslant\max_{0\leqslant n\leqslant m}Lr^{n}s^{m-n}\leqslant Ls^{m}

since
r<s
. Therefore
|w_{m}|_{V}=\max\{|w_{m}|_{V(f/s)},|w_{m}|_{V(s/f)}\}\leqslant Ls^{m}
and hence
\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}w_{m}T^{m}
has radius of convergence at least
1/s
. ∎

We’re now ready for our next local calculation, dealing with the critical case when
|\mathcal{S}|=1
. Note that, in this case, if
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
and
a_{1}\not\in X(K)
then
X\subseteq U_{t}
since if
X
splits over
K^{\prime}
then
\rho(X_{K^{\prime}})\geqslant t
. Thus, given Proposition
4.2.12
, we might as well assume
a_{1}\in X(K)
.

Theorem 4.2.16
.

Suppose that
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
and
a_{1}\in X(K)
. Suppose also that
h=1
and write
a:=a_{1}
and
k=v_{a}(u)
so that
u=(x-a)^{k}
. Then the complex

0\to\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot z}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)\to 0

is exact whenever
\frac{k}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

This is based on
[
12
, Proposition 5.1.2(ii)]
, which Berthelot attributes to Laumon. Recall that the sequence is a complex follows from Lemma
4.2.11
. Note also that
R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=R(u,d)
since
k\neq 0
.

First we prove that the last non-zero map is surjective. To the end we consider a typical element
P=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}P_{n}\partial_{x}^{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
with
P_{n}\in\mathcal{O}(X)
. The required convergence condition is that there is some
r>\varpi/t
such that
|P_{n}|r^{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. We furthermore compute that

(30)

P\cdot z=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}n!P_{n}(x-a)^{-n}\hskip 2.84544ptz.

Take an element
g\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)(X)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
. Using
(\ref{OvConvMudSects}
), we can find some
s\in\sqrt{|K|^{\times}}
with
s<t
such that
g=fz
for some
f\in\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})
. Since
\mathcal{O}(X\cap U_{s})=\mathcal{O}(X)\langle s/(x-a)\rangle
, we can find a sequence
(b_{n})
in
\mathcal{O}(X)
such that

f=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}(x-a)^{-n}\quad\mbox{with}\quad\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}|b_{n}|s^{-n}=0.

Thus to prove surjectivity, it suffices to show that if
|b_{n}|s^{-n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for some
s<t
, then
\left|\frac{b_{n}}{n!}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}^{-1}\right|r^{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for some
r>\varpi/t
.

Now as noted in
[
30
, Proposition 13.1.5, Corollary 13.1.7]
, the rational number
\frac{k}{d}
is
p
-adic non-Liouville and so the radius of convergence of the series

\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{T^{n}}{n!}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}^{-1}

is at least
\varpi
; that is
\sup_{n\geqslant 0}\left|\frac{1}{n!}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}^{-1}\right|w^{n}<\infty
for all
w<\varpi
. Now, because
s<t
, we can choose
w
such that
\varpi s/t<w<\varpi
. Then
r:=w/s>\varpi/t
works.

Next we prove the injectivity of the first non-zero map. From Definition
4.2.10
(b) we can see that

R(u,d)=(x-a)\partial_{x}-\frac{k}{d}.

It follows from this that for every
Q=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{n}\partial_{x}^{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
we have

(31)

Q\cdot R(u,d)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left((n-\frac{k}{d})Q_{n}+(x-a)Q_{n-1}\right)\partial_{x}^{n}

and so if
Q\cdot R(u,d)=0
then

(n-\frac{k}{d})Q_{n}+(x-a)Q_{n-1}=0\quad\mbox{for all}\quad n\geqslant 0,

where
a_{-1}:=0
. Since
\frac{k}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}
, an easy recurrence gives that
a_{n}=0
for all
n
.

It remains to show that the kernel of
\cdot z
is contained in the image of
\cdot R(u,d)
. So we suppose that
P=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}P_{n}\partial_{x}^{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
satisfies
P\cdot z=0
, and deduce using (
30
) that

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}n!P_{n}(x-a)^{-n}=0\in\mathcal{O}(X)_{\overline{U_{t}}}\subset\mathcal{O}\left(X\left(\frac{t}{x-a}\right)\right).

Let
b_{m}:=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{m}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}n!P_{n}(x-a)^{m-n}
. Because
\frac{k}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}
by assumption, we can define

Q:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{c_{n}}{n!}\partial_{x}^{n}\quad\mbox{where}\quad c_{n}:=\left[\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}(n-\frac{k}{d})\right]^{-1}b_{n}\quad\mbox{for all}\quad n\geqslant 0.

We wish to show that
Q\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
. Granted this, it is an easy computation, using (
31
), to check that
Q\cdot R(u,d)=P
giving the result.

Since
P\in\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
, there is some
r>\varpi/t
such that
|P_{n}|r^{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Moreover, for any
s>\varpi
,
|n!|/s^{n}
is bounded by Lemma
2.3.16
and
\left|\binom{k/d}{n}\right|\leqslant 1
for all
n
since
p\nmid d
. Thus, choosing
s\in(\varpi,tr)
, we see that
\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\binom{k/d}{n}n!P_{n}T^{n}
has radius of convergence at least
r/s>1/t
in
\mathcal{O}(X)[[T]]
. Now we may appeal to Lemma
4.2.15
, with
f=x-a
and
v_{n}=\binom{k/d}{n}{n!}P_{n}
to see that
\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}b_{m}T^{m}
has radius of convergence greater than
1/t
. Since
\frac{k}{d}
is non-Liouville,
\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{T^{n}}{\frac{k}{d}-n}
has radius of convergence
1
and as noted above
\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{T^{n}}{n!}\binom{\frac{k}{d}}{n}^{-1}
has radius of convergence
\varpi
. Combining all of these estimates, we see that there is some
u>\varpi/t
such that
|\frac{c_{n}}{n!}|u^{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
as required.
∎

Next, we collect several basic facts about the linear differential operator
R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)
.

Lemma 4.2.17
.

(1)

R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}\theta_{u,d}(\partial_{x})
.

(2)

R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=\theta_{u\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}^{d},d}(\partial_{x})\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}
.

(3)

Let
v\in K(x)
with
\mathcal{S}(v)\subset\mathcal{S}
. Then

R_{\mathcal{S}}(uv,d)=\theta_{u,d}(R_{\mathcal{S}}(v,d)).

Proof.

(a) This follows directly from Definition
4.2.10
(b) and Lemma
2.4.1
.

(b) For any
v\in\mathcal{O}(X)^{\times}
,
\theta_{v^{d},d}
is conjugation by
v
in
\mathcal{D}(X)
by Lemma
2.4.1
. Applying Lemma
2.4.3
together with part (a), we see that

\theta_{u\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}^{d},d}(\partial_{x})\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}=\theta_{\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}^{d},d}\left(\theta_{u,d}(\partial_{x})\right)\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}\theta_{u,d}(\partial_{x})=R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d).

(c) We can compute

R_{\mathcal{S}}(uv,d)=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}\theta_{uv,d}(\partial_{x})=\Delta_{\mathcal{S}}\theta_{u,d}(\theta_{v,d}(\partial_{x}))=\theta_{u,d}(R_{\mathcal{S}}(v,d))

using part (a), Lemma
2.4.3
and the fact that
\theta_{u,d}
is
\mathcal{O}(X)
-linear.
∎

Here is our final local calculation.

Proposition 4.2.18
.

Let
Y\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
containing at most one
a_{i}
. Then

0\to\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot z}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)\to 0.

is exact, provided that
v_{a_{i}}(u)/d\notin\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

If none of the
a_{i}
live in
Y
then we’re done by Proposition
4.2.12
. So we may
suppose
a_{i}\in Y(K)
and define
a:=a_{i}
,
k:=v_{a_{i}}(u)
and
v=u/(x-a)^{k}
so that
v
is a unit in
\mathcal{O}(Y)
. In particular
\theta_{v,d}
induces an automorphism of
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)
by Corollary
2.4.11
. Moreover by Lemma
4.2.17
(c),

R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=\theta_{v,d}(R_{\mathcal{S}}((x-a)^{k},d)).

Thus
\mathcal{D}^{{\dagger}}_{\varpi/t}(Y)R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)=\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\theta_{v,d}(R_{\mathcal{S}}((x-a)^{k},d))
. Under
\theta^{-1}_{v,d}
the
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)
-module
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)
corresponds to the
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)
-module
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},(x-a)^{k},d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)
. Now we can apply Theorem
4.2.16
.
∎

Definition 4.2.19
.

For
i=1,\ldots,h
let
W_{i}
be the affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{A}
whose
\mathbf{C}
-points are obtained by removing the open discs
\{z\in{\bf C}\mid|z-a_{j}|<t\}
for
j\neq i
from the closed disc
\{z\in{\bf C}\mid|z-a_{i}|\leqslant t\}
.

We note that the condition
t\leqslant\min\limits_{i\neq j}|a_{i}-a_{j}|
means that each
W_{i}
contains
a_{i}
but does not contain
a_{j}
for
i\neq j
.

Lemma 4.2.20
.

(1)

Each
W_{i}
is
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
-admissible.

(2)

Let
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
, and suppose that

t\leqslant\min\{|a_{i}-a_{j}|:a_{i},a_{j}\in X({\bf C})\quad\mbox{and}\quad i\neq j\}.

Then
\{X\cap W_{1},\ldots,X\cap W_{h},X\cap U_{t}\}
is an admissible covering of
X
.

Proof.

(a) We choose a finite field extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
such that
t\in|K^{\prime}|
; then
W_{i}\times_{K}K^{\prime}
becomes a cheese such that
\rho(W_{i}\times_{K}K^{\prime})=t
. Hence

r(W_{i})=r(W_{i}\times_{K}K^{\prime})=\varpi/t

by Lemma
2.3.13
and Corollary
2.3.17
.

(b) Applying Lemma
2.3.13
and Corollary
2.3.17
again shows

\rho(X\times_{K}{{\bf C}})=\varpi/r(X\times_{K}{\bf C})\geqslant\frac{\varpi}{\varpi/t}=t.

Suppose that
a\in X({\bf C})
and
|a-a_{i}|<t
for some
i
. If
a_{i}\notin X({\bf C})
, then because
\rho(X\times_{K}{\bf{C}})\geqslant t
, the intersection of
X({\bf C})
with the open disc of radius
t
around
a_{i}
is empty. But
|a-a_{i}|<t
and
a\in X({\bf C})
— contradiction — so in fact
a_{i}\in X({\bf C})
whenever
a\in X({\bf C})
and
|a-a_{i}|<t
. We will now show that

X({\bf C})=(X\cap U_{t})({\bf C})\cup(X\cap W_{1})({\bf C})\cup\cdots\cup(X\cap W_{h}({\bf C}).

Indeed, if
a\in X({\bf C})-(X\cap U_{t})({\bf C})
then
a\in X(\bf C)
and
|a-a_{i}|<t
for some
i
, so that
a_{i}\in X({\bf C})
by the above. But if also
|a-a_{j}|<t
for some
j\neq i
, then
a_{j}\in X({\bf C})
by the above as well, and then

|a_{i}-a_{j}|\leqslant\max\{|a-a_{i}|,|a-a_{j}|\}<t

contradicts our hypothesis on
t
. So in fact
|a-a_{j}|\geqslant t
for all
j\neq i
which forces
a\in(X\cap W_{i})(\bf{C})
and establishes our claim. Since
\{X\cap U_{t},X\cap W_{1},\ldots,X\cap W_{h}\}
is a finite set of affinoid subdomains of
X
, which covers
X
on
{\bf C}
-points, we conclude that it is an admissible covering of
X
.
∎

Here is the main result of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{KummerSect}
.

Theorem 4.2.21
.

Let
X\in\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
. Suppose that

•

t\in\sqrt{|K^{\times}|}
and
p\nmid d
,

•

t\leqslant\min\{|a_{i}-a_{j}|:a_{i},a_{j}\in X({\bf C})\quad\mbox{and}\quad i\neq j\}
, and

•

k_{i}/d\not\in\mathbb{N}
whenever
a_{i}\in X({\bf C})
.

Then the complex of
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)
-modules

0\to\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(X)\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(X)\to 0

from Lemma
4.2.11
is exact.

Proof.

Lemma
4.2.20
(b) tells us that
\{X\cap U_{t},X\cap W_{1},\ldots,X\cap W_{h}\}
is an
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
-admissible covering of
X
. By Theorem
2.3.10
and Theorem
4.1.9
, we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}
and
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}
are sheaves on
\mathbb{A}(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
with vanishing higher Čech cohomology. Therefore it suffices to show that the sequence

0\to\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}}(u,d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/t}(Y)\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)_{\overline{U_{t}}}(Y)\to 0

is exact whenever
Y
is an intersection of any members of our covering; note that this
Y
is still
(t\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
-admissible. Since each member of our covering contains at most one
a_{i}
the exactness follows from Proposition
4.2.18
.∎

Finally, we record a purely algebraic calculation which tells us how the the relators
R_{\mathcal{S}(u)}(u,d)
from Definition
4.2.10
interact with the maps
g^{\mathcal{D}}:\mathcal{D}\to g^{\ast}\mathcal{D}
defining the
G
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{D}
. Recall from
\lx@sectionsign\ref{AffTrans}
that
\mathbb{B}
denotes the subgroup scheme
\{g\in\mathbb{GL}_{2}:g_{21}=0\}
of upper-triangular matrices in
\mathbb{GL}_{2}
. Recall also the isomorphism
g^{\dagger}_{r}\colon\mathcal{D}_{r}^{\dagger}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}g^{\ast}\mathcal{D}_{\frac{r}{\varrho(g)}}^{\dagger}
from Corollary
2.5.6
(b).

Lemma 4.2.22
.

Let
g\in\mathbb{B}(K)
, let
W
be an affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{A}
and let
u\in K(x)\cap\mathcal{O}(W)^{\times}
.

(1)

We have
g\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}(u)}(u,d)=\varrho(g)^{1-|\mathcal{S}(u)|}\hskip 2.84544ptR_{\mathcal{S}(g\cdot u)}(g\cdot u,d)
.

(2)

Suppose that
W\in\mathbb{A}(\partial_{x}/r)^{\dagger}
for some
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. Then

g^{\dagger}_{r}(W)\left(R_{\mathcal{S}(u)}(u,d)\right)=\varrho(g)^{1-|\mathcal{S}(u)|}\ R_{\mathcal{S}(g\cdot u)}(g\cdot u,d).

Proof.

(a) By Lemma
2.5.2
(e), we have
g\cdot(x-z)=\varrho(g)^{-1}(x-g\cdot z)
for any
z\in\overline{K}
. Therefore
v_{g\cdot z}(g\cdot u)=v_{z}(u)
, so
\mathcal{S}(g\cdot u)=g\cdot\mathcal{S}(u)
and

g\cdot\Delta_{u}=\prod_{z\in\mathcal{S}(u)}\varrho(g)(x-g\cdot z)=\varrho(g)^{-|\mathcal{S}(u)|}\Delta_{g\cdot u}.

Because we also have
g\cdot\partial_{x}=\varrho(g)\partial_{x}
by Lemma
2.5.2
(e), Lemma
2.5.7
gives

\begin{array}[]{lll}g\cdot R_{\mathcal{S}(u)}(u,d)&=&g\cdot\Delta_{u}\hskip 2.84544ptg\cdot\theta_{u,d}(\partial_{x})=\varrho(g)^{-|\mathcal{S}(u)|}\Delta_{g\cdot u}\hskip 2.84544pt\theta_{g\cdot u,d}(g\cdot\partial_{x})\\
&=&\varrho(g)^{1-|\mathcal{S}(u)|}R_{\mathcal{S}(g\cdot u)}(g\cdot u,d).\end{array}\qed

(b) This follows from part (a) and the commutative square (
13
).

4.3.
Line bundles with connection on the local Drinfeld space

In
\lx@sectionsign\ref{UpperHP}
, we work with the following data.

Notation 4.3.1
.

(1)

\Omega:=\mathbb{P}^{1,\an}-\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)
is the the Drinfeld upper half plane,

(2)

\Upsilon=\mathbb{D}\cap\Omega
,

(3)

j\colon\Upsilon\hookrightarrow\mathbb{D}
is the open embedding,

(4)

[\mathscr{L}]\in\PicCon(\Upsilon)[p^{\prime}]
,

(5)

d
is the order of
[\mathscr{L}]
in
\PicCon(\Upsilon)
. Thus
d
is coprime to
p
.

We refer the reader to
[
8
, Definition 3.2.1]
for the terminology, and we fix a
\mathcal{D}
-linear isomorphism
\psi\colon\mathscr{L}^{\otimes d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon}
.

Definition 4.3.2
.

Let
n\geqslant 0
be an integer.

(1)

V_{n}:=\mathbb{A}\backslash\bigcup\limits_{a\in\mathcal{O}_{F}}\{|z-a|<|\pi_{F}|^{n}\}
,

(2)

\Upsilon_{n}:=V_{n}\cap\mathbb{D}
,

(3)

\mathscr{L}_{n}:=j_{\ast}(\mathscr{L}_{\overline{\Upsilon_{n}}})
, and

(4)

h_{n}:=q^{n+1}
.

See Definition
4.1.1
for the meaning of the sheaf
\mathscr{L}_{\overline{\Upsilon_{n}}}
on
\Upsilon
.

We note that if
\mathcal{S}:=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{h_{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
is any set of coset representatives for the group
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
, then
V_{n}
was denoted
U(\mathcal{S})_{|\pi_{F}|^{n}}
in §
4.2
.

We also note that
\Upsilon_{n}
is the cheese obtained by removing
h_{n}
open balls of radius
|\pi_{F}|^{n}
from
\mathbb{D}
with centres at
a_{1},\ldots,a_{h_{n}}
and
\{\Upsilon_{n}\}_{n\geqslant 0}
forms an admissible cover of
\Upsilon
totally ordered by inclusion.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

The maps
j_{\ast}\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{L}_{n}
given by restriction induce an isomorphism
j_{\ast}\mathscr{L}\cong\mathop{\underset{\scriptstyle}{\underset{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.75346pt][-1.50694pt]{$\longleftarrow$}}{\operatorname{lim}}}}\mathscr{L}_{n}
of
\mathcal{D}
-modules on
\mathbb{D}
.

Proof.

Since
j_{\ast}
is a right adjoint it commutes with limits, and so it suffices to show that the restriction maps induce an isomorphism
\mathscr{L}\cong\mathop{\underset{\scriptstyle}{\underset{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.75346pt][-1.50694pt]{$\longleftarrow$}}{\operatorname{lim}}}}\mathscr{L}_{\overline{\Upsilon_{n}}}
of
\mathcal{D}
-modules on
\Upsilon
. Since the
\Upsilon_{n}
forms an admissible cover of
\Upsilon
which is totally ordered by inclusion, by Proposition
4.1.3
, the restriction maps induce such an isomorphism of abelian sheaves on
\Upsilon
. It is easy to see that all relevant maps are
\mathcal{D}
-linear.
∎

Note that there is a natural bijection

h(\Upsilon_{n})\backslash\{D_{\infty}\}\to\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}

sending
D
to
D\cap\mathcal{O}_{F}
for each
D\in h(\Upsilon_{n})\backslash\{D_{\infty}\}
.

Notation 4.3.4
.

For each
a\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
and
n\geqslant 0
, let
D_{a,n}
be the element of
h(\Upsilon_{n})\backslash\{D_{\infty}\}
corresponding to
a+\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
under this bijection.

We introduce the natural map abelian groups

M_{n,d}\colon\PicCon(\Upsilon)[d]\to M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{n}),\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z})

obtained by composing the restriction map

\PicCon(\Upsilon)[d]\to\Con(\Upsilon_{n})[d];\hskip 14.22636pt\mathscr{L}\mapsto\mathscr{L}|_{\Upsilon_{n}}

with the isomorphisms
\theta_{d}
and
\mu_{\Upsilon_{n},d}
from
[
8
, Definition 3.1.15 and Corollary 4.3.3]
:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 28.23572pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.23572pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Con(\Upsilon_{n})[d]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.8734pt\raise-6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.49464pt\raise 5.9611pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta_{d}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.23572pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.23572pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 82.23572pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\frac{\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times}}{K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times d}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 182.86516pt\raise-6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 174.9313pt\raise 5.5236pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.4903pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu_{\Upsilon_{n},d}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 217.7579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 187.7579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 217.7579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{0}\left(h(\Upsilon_{n}),\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{d\mathbb{Z}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For each
n\geqslant 0
, there is a unique
\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z}
-valued measure on
h(\Upsilon_{n})
of total value zero, whose value on each
D_{a,n}
is
1
:

\nu_{n}(D_{a,n})=1+d\mathbb{Z}\quad\mbox{for all}\quad a\in\mathcal{O}_{F},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nu_{n}(D_{\infty})=-q^{n+1}+d\mathbb{Z}.

Lemma 4.3.5
.

Let
n\geqslant 0
.

(1)

M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{n}),\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z})^{I}
is freely generated as a
\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z}
-module by
\nu_{n}
.

(2)

The restriction map
r_{n+1}:M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{n+1}),\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z})^{I}\to M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{n}),\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z})^{I}
sends
\nu_{n+1}
to
q\nu_{n}
.

(3)

r_{n+1}
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

(4)

The restriction map
\PicCon(\Upsilon)^{I}[d]\to\Con(\Upsilon_{n})^{I}[d]
is an isomorphism.

(5)

\PicCon(\Upsilon)^{I}[d]
is a free
\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z}
-module of rank
1
.

Proof.

(a) The natural
I
-action on
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}\cup\{\infty\}
has two orbits:
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
and
\{\infty\}
. Therefore the natural
I
-action on
h(\Upsilon_{n})
also has two orbits, namely
\{D_{b,n}:b\in\mathcal{O}_{F}\}
and
\{D_{\infty}\}
. If
\nu
is an
I
-invariant measure on
h(\Upsilon_{n})
, then it must be constant on these orbits. If, in addition, it has total value zero, then we easily see that
\nu=\nu(D_{0,n})\nu_{n}
as required.

(b) Fix
b\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
. In view of part (a), the map
r_{n+1}
must send
\nu_{n+1}
to some integer multiple of
\nu_{n}
. However the restriction map
h(\Upsilon_{n+1})\to h(\Upsilon_{n})
sends
D_{b+\pi_{F}^{n+2}c,n+1}
to
D_{b,n}
for each
c\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
, and the preimage of
D_{b,n}
under this map consists of precisely
q
such holes. Hence
r_{n+1}(\nu_{n+1})=q\nu_{n}
.

(c) This follows immediately from parts (a) and (b) since
d
and
q
are coprime.

(d) For each
m\geqslant n
there is a natural commutative square

\textstyle{\Con(\Upsilon_{m+1})^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\Upsilon_{m+1},d}\circ\theta_{d}}
\textstyle{\Con(\Upsilon_{m})^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\Upsilon_{m},d}\circ\theta_{d}}
\textstyle{M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{m+1}),\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z})^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{m+1}}
\textstyle{M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{m}),\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z})^{I}}

whose vertical arrows are isomorphisms by
[
8
, Corollary 4.3.4]
. Using part (b) we see that the top horizontal arrow is bijective. Hence the connecting maps in the inverse system
(\Con(\Upsilon_{m})^{I})_{m\geqslant n}
are all isomorphisms, so the projection map

\mathop{\underset{\scriptstyle}{\underset{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.75346pt][-1.50694pt]{$\longleftarrow$}}{\operatorname{lim}}}}\Con(\Upsilon_{m})^{I}\to\Con(\Upsilon_{n})^{I}

is an isomorphism as well. Next, we have
\PicCon(\Upsilon_{m})=\Con(\Upsilon_{m})
by
[
8
, Proposition 4.1.11]
. The family
(\Upsilon_{m})_{m\geqslant n}
forms an increasing admissible covering of
\Upsilon
by geometrically connected affinoid subdomains, so
[
8
, Proposition 3.1.9]
implies that the restriction map

\PicCon(\Upsilon)^{I}\longrightarrow\mathop{\underset{\scriptstyle}{\underset{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.75346pt][-1.50694pt]{$\longleftarrow$}}{\operatorname{lim}}}}\PicCon(\Upsilon_{m})^{I}

is an isomorphism. The composition of this restriction map with the projection
\PicCon(\Upsilon_{m})^{I}\to\PicCon(\Upsilon_{n})^{I}
is the restriction map
\PicCon(\Upsilon)^{I}\to\PicCon(\Upsilon_{n})^{I}
in the statement of (d), and it is therefore an isomorphism as required.

(e) Use part (d),
[
8
, Corollary 4.3.4]
and part (a).
∎

Definition 4.3.6
.

Let
X
be an affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{D}
. A section
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}_{n}(X)
is said to be an
algebraic generator
if there exists
u\in K(x)
such that

(1)

\mathscr{L}_{n|X}=(\mathcal{O}_{X})_{\overline{X\cap V_{n}}}\dot{z}
,

(2)

\psi(\dot{z}^{\otimes d})=u
,

(3)

\mathcal{S}(u)\subset F
(see Definition
4.2.1
for this notation),

(4)

|a-b|\geqslant|\pi_{F}|^{n}
for all
a\neq b\in\mathcal{S}(u)\cap X
, and

(5)

\frac{v_{a}(u)}{d}\not\in\mathbb{N}
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}(u)\cap X
.

We call
u
the
associated rational function
.

We will now prove that algebraic generators exist.

Theorem 4.3.7
.

Let
X
be an affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{D}
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
be any set of coset representatives for the group
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
and let
(k_{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
be a family of integers such that for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
we have

(32)

\frac{k_{a}}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}\quad\mbox{and}\quad M_{n,d}([\mathscr{L}])(D_{a,n})={k_{a}}+d\mathbb{Z}.

Then there exists an algebraic generator
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}_{n}(X)
with associated rational function of the form

u=\lambda\prod_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(x-a)^{k_{a}}\quad\mbox{for some}\quad\lambda\in K^{\times}.

(2)

Algebraic generators always exist.

Proof.

(a) Using Lemma
4.1.2
we can see that if
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mathbb{D})
is algebraic generator then
\dot{z}|_{X}
is an algebraic generator with the same associated rational function and so we only need to consider the case
X=\mathbb{D}
itself.

Let
u_{0}:=\prod\limits_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(x-a)^{k_{a}}\in\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times}
. Then the assumption (
32
) tells us that

\mu_{\Upsilon_{n},d}(u_{0}K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times d})=M_{n,d}([\mathscr{L}|)=\mu_{\Upsilon_{n},d}(\theta_{d}(\mathscr{L}|_{\Upsilon_{n}})).

Since
\mu_{\Upsilon_{n},d}
is an isomorphism, we see that

(33)

\theta_{d}(\mathscr{L}|_{\Upsilon_{n}})=u_{0}K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times d}.

Write
\mathcal{S}=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{h_{n}}\}
, choose an integer
\ell
coprime to
p
and define

V:=\Sp K\left\langle x,\frac{\pi_{F}^{\ell n+1}}{(x-a_{1})^{\ell}},\cdots,\frac{\pi_{F}^{\ell n+1}}{(x-a_{h_{n}})^{\ell}}\right\rangle.

This is a
K
-form of the
\mathbf{C}
-cheese
V_{\mathbf{C}}
obtained from
\mathbb{D}_{\mathbf{C}}
by removing the open balls of radius
|\pi_{F}|^{n+\frac{1}{\ell}}
around the
a_{i}
. Observe that
V
is a wide open neighbourhood of
\Upsilon_{n}
contained in
\Upsilon_{n+1}
. Since
V
and
\Upsilon_{n}
are geometrically connected, we have the following commutative square whose vertical arrows are given by restriction:

\textstyle{\Con(V)[d]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}/K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{n}}
\textstyle{\Con(\Upsilon_{n})[d]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times}/K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times d}.}

We claim that
r_{n}
is injective. Assuming this, and noting that
u_{0}
also lies in
\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}
, it follows from (
33
) that
\theta_{d}([\mathscr{L}|_{V}])=u_{0}K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}
inside
\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}/K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}
. Using the construction of
\theta_{d}
given at
[
8
, Proposition 3.1.14]
, we can find a section
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}(V)
and some
\lambda\in K^{\times}
such that
\mathscr{L}(V)=\mathcal{O}(V)\dot{z}
and
\psi(\dot{z}^{\otimes d})=u:=\lambda u_{0}
. Since
V
is affinoid and
\mathscr{L}
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}
-module, in fact we also have
\mathscr{L}_{|V}=\mathcal{O}_{|V}\dot{z}
. Since
V
is a wide open neighbourhood of
\Upsilon_{n}
, i.e.
\widetilde{V}
contains the closure of
\widetilde{\Upsilon_{n}}
, we may view
\dot{z}
as an element of
\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mathbb{D})
and deduce that
\mathscr{L}_{n|\mathbb{D}}=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})_{\overline{\Upsilon_{n}}}\dot{z}
.

Finally,
v_{a}(u)=k_{a}
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
. As none of the
k_{a}
can be zero, given the assumption that
\frac{k_{a}}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
, we have
\mathcal{S}(u)=\mathcal{S}
and this is contained in
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. We now see that Definition
4.3.6
(c,d,e) is satisfied and
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mathbb{D})
is an algebraic generator, once we have checked the injectivity of
r_{n}
claimed above.

Let
L=K(\pi_{F}^{\frac{1}{\ell}})
. Then in the notation of
[
8
, Definition 4.1.1]
,
V_{L}
is isomorphic to the
L
-cheese
C_{L}(0,a_{1},\cdots,a_{h_{n}},1,\pi_{F}^{n+\frac{1}{\ell}},\cdots,\pi_{F}^{n+\frac{1}{\ell}})
as an
L
-affinoid variety. The map
r_{n}
appears in the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}/K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{n}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times}/L^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{V_{L},d}}
\textstyle{M_{0}(h(V_{L}),\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{d\mathbb{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times}/K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n})^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n,L})^{\times}/L^{\times}\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon_{n,L})^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\Upsilon_{n,L},d}}
\textstyle{M_{0}(h(\Upsilon_{n,L}),\frac{\mathbb{Z}}{d\mathbb{Z}}).}

Since
|h(V_{L})|=|h(\Upsilon_{n,L})|=h_{n}
, the vertical arrow on the right is bijective. Since
\mu_{V_{L},d}
is an isomorphism by
[
8
, Corollary 4.3.3(c)]
, it remains to show that the first horizontal arrow in the top row is injective, or equivalently, that

L^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times d}\hskip 2.84544pt\cap\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}\subseteq K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}.

Let
S
denote the subgroup of
\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}
generated by
x-a_{1},\cdots,x-a_{h_{n}}
. View
S
also as a subgroup of
\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times}
. Applying
[
8
, Proposition 4.1.10]
to
V_{L}
, we have

\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times}=\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times\times}\cdot L^{\times}\cdot S.

Now
L^{\times}=L^{\times\times}\cdot K^{\times}\cdot\pi_{F}^{\frac{1}{\ell}\mathbb{Z}}
because
L=K(\pi_{F}^{\frac{1}{\ell}})
is a totally ramified extension of
K
. Using
[
8
, Lemma 4.3.2(a)]
we then see that

L^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times d}=\pi^{\frac{d}{\ell}\mathbb{Z}}\cdot\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times\times}\cdot K^{\times d}\cdot S^{d}.

Since
K^{\times d}S^{d}\subseteq K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}
, by the modular law it remains to show that

\pi_{F}^{\frac{d}{\ell}\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times\times}\cap\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}\subseteq K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}.

Let
\zeta\in\overline{K}
be a primitive
\ell^{th}
root of unity and let
M:=L(\zeta)
, a Galois extension of
K
with Galois group
\mathcal{G}
. Let
u\in\pi_{F}^{\frac{d}{\ell}\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\times\times}\cap\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times}
. Then
u
is fixed by the natural action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{O}(V_{M})
and we can write
u=\pi_{F}^{\frac{m}{\ell}}(1+\epsilon)
for some
m\in d\mathbb{Z}
and
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\circ\circ}
. Choose
\sigma\in\mathcal{G}
which sends
\pi_{F}^{\frac{1}{\ell}}
to
\zeta\pi_{F}^{\frac{1}{\ell}}
; then

\sigma(\pi_{F}^{\frac{m}{\ell}})(1+\sigma(\epsilon))=\pi_{F}^{\frac{m}{\ell}}(1+\epsilon),\quad\mbox{so}\quad\zeta^{m}=\frac{\sigma(\pi_{F}^{\frac{m}{\ell}})}{\pi_{F}^{\frac{m}{\ell}}}=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1+\sigma(\epsilon)}\in\mathcal{O}(V_{M})^{\times\times}.

Since
p\neq\ell
and
(\zeta^{m})^{\ell}=1
, it follows from
[
8
, Lemma 4.3.2(a)]
that
\zeta^{m}=1
. We conclude that
\ell\mid m
. Therefore
\pi_{F}^{\frac{m}{\ell}}\in K^{\times}
and
\epsilon=\pi_{F}^{-\frac{m}{\ell}}u-1\in\mathcal{O}(V)
. Since
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}(V_{L})^{\circ\circ}
, we see that
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}(V)^{\circ\circ}
and
u\in K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times\times}
. Applying
[
8
, Lemma 4.3.2(a)]
again shows that
u\in K^{\times}\mathcal{O}(V)^{\times d}
and completes the proof.

(b) Choose any set
\mathcal{S}
of coset representatives for
\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
in
\mathcal{O}_{F}
, and choose any family
(\ell_{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
of integers such that
M_{n,d}([\mathscr{L}])(a+\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F})=\frac{\ell_{a}}{d}+\mathbb{Z}
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
. Let
M
be any positive integer such that
M>\max_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\ell_{a}}{d}
and let
k_{a}:=\ell_{a}-Md<0
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
. Then
\frac{k_{a}}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}
and
\frac{\ell_{a}}{d}+\mathbb{Z}=\frac{k_{a}}{d}+\mathbb{Z}
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
. We can now apply part (a) to the family of integers
(k_{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
to conclude.∎

We recall the Definition
4.2.2
of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S},u,d)
for any
u\in K(x)
with
\mathcal{S}(u)\subset\mathcal{S}
.

Proposition 4.3.8
.

Let
X
be an affinoid subdomain of
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathcal{S}_{n}
be a set of coset representatives for the group
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi_{F}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Moreover, let
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}_{n}(X)
be an algebraic generator, with associated rational function
u_{n}
such that
\mathcal{S}(u_{n})\subset\mathcal{S}_{n}
. There is a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-linear isomorphism

\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S}_{n},u_{n},d)_{\overline{V_{n}}}\right)_{|X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{L}_{n|X}

which sends
z
to
\dot{z}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.2.5
applied with
t=|\pi_{F}|^{n}
, and by Lemma
4.1.2
, we have

\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S}_{n},u_{n},d)_{\overline{V_{n}}}\right)|_{X}=\left((\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}})_{\overline{V_{n}}}\right)|_{X}z=(\mathcal{O}_{X})_{\overline{V_{n}\cap X}}z.

By Definition
4.3.6
(a) we know that
\mathscr{L}_{n|X}=(\mathcal{O}_{X})_{\overline{X\cap V_{n}}}\dot{z}
. Hence sending
z
to
\dot{z}
defines an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear isomorphism

\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S}_{n},u_{n},d)_{\overline{V_{n}}}\right)_{|X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{L}_{n|X}.

The fact that
\psi(\dot{z}^{\otimes d})=u_{n}
implies that this isomorphism is in fact
\mathcal{D}
-linear.
∎

We are now in the situation of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{KummerSect}
and can begin to reap the rewards.

Definition 4.3.9
.

Let
n\geqslant 0
.

(1)

\mathbb{D}_{n}
will denote the
G
-topology
\mathbb{D}_{n}:=\mathbb{D}(|\pi_{F}|^{n}\partial_{x}/\varpi)^{\dagger}
on
\mathbb{D}
.

(2)

\mathscr{D}_{n}
will denote the sheaf
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi/|\pi_{F}|^{n}}
on
\mathbb{D}_{n}
.

In view of Corollary
2.3.17
and Lemma
2.3.13
, we see that an affinoid subdomain
X
of
\mathbb{D}
lies in
\mathbb{D}_{n}
if and only if
\rho(X_{\mathbf{C}})\geqslant|\pi_{F}|^{n}
. We note the following flatness properties that follow from work done in §
3
.

Lemma 4.3.10
.

(1)

\mathscr{D}_{n}(X)
is a flat right
\mathscr{D}_{n+1}(X)
-module whenever
X\in\mathbb{D}_{n+1}
.

(2)

\mathscr{D}_{n}(Y)
is a flat right
\mathscr{D}_{n}(X)
-module whenever
Y\subseteq X
in
\mathbb{D}_{n}
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Theorem
3.2.1
.
∎

Corollary 4.3.11
.

The natural action of the sheaf of finite order differential operators
\mathcal{D}
on the restriction of
\mathscr{L}_{n}
to
\mathbb{D}_{n}
extends to
\mathscr{D}_{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{n}
be any set of coset representatives for the group
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\pi^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Theorem
4.3.7
and Proposition
4.3.8
together give us a
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{D}}
-linear isomorphism

\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S}_{n},u_{n},d)_{\overline{V_{n}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{L}_{n}.

We can now apply Proposition
4.2.9
with
t=|\pi_{F}|^{n}
to transport the
\mathscr{D}_{n}
-action on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S}_{n},u_{n},d)_{\overline{V_{n}}}
to
\mathscr{L}_{n}
along this isomorphism. ∎

Recall the relator
R_{\mathcal{S}(u)}(u,d)
from Definition
4.2.10
(b).

Corollary 4.3.12
.

Let
X\in\mathbb{D}_{n}
and let
\dot{z}\in\mathscr{L}_{n}(X)
be an algebraic generator, with associated rational function
u_{n}
. Then
\mathscr{L}_{n}(X)
is a finitely presented
\mathscr{D}_{n}(X)
-module:

\mathscr{L}_{n}(X)=\mathscr{D}_{n}(X)\cdot\dot{z}\cong\mathscr{D}_{n}(X)/\mathscr{D}_{n}(X)R_{\mathcal{S}(u_{n})}(u_{n},d).

Proof.

By Proposition
4.3.8
, there is a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-linear isomorphism

\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S}(u_{n}),u_{n},d)_{\overline{V_{n}}}\right)_{|X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathscr{L}_{n})_{|X}

sending
z
to
\dot{z}
. Note that
|a-b|\geqslant|\pi_{F}|^{n}
for all
a\neq b\in\mathcal{S}(u_{n})\cap X
by Definition
4.3.6
(d) and
\frac{v_{a}(u_{n})}{d}\notin\mathbb{N}
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}(u_{n})\cap X
by Definition
4.3.6
(e). Now we may apply Theorem
4.2.21
with
t=|\pi_{F}|^{n}
.
∎

4.4.
Local irreducibility

We assume throughout
\lx@sectionsign\ref{LocIrred}
that our ground field
K
is discretely valued, with uniformiser
\pi_{K}
and residue field
k
. Let
X
be an affinoid subdomain of the closed unit disc
\mathbb{D}
obtained by removing finitely many open discs of radius
1

not
containing the point
x=0
. Thus

X=\Sp K\left\langle x,\frac{1}{x-\alpha_{1}},\cdots,\frac{1}{x-\alpha_{v}}\right\rangle

for some
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{v}\in K
with
|\alpha_{1}|=\cdots=|\alpha_{v}|=1
. We also consider the case where
v=0
when
X=\mathbb{D}
. The main result of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{LocIrred}
is the following

Theorem 4.4.1
.

Let
\lambda\in K\backslash\mathbb{Z}
and let
X
be as above. Then the
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi}(X)
-module
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi}(X)/\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\varpi}(X)(x\partial-\lambda)
is simple if
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and zero otherwise.

Let
\mathscr{X}:=\Spf\mathcal{O}(X)^{\circ}
and note that
\mathscr{X}
is the formal completion of the punctured affine line
\Spec K^{\circ}\left[x,\frac{1}{x-\alpha_{1}},\cdots,\frac{1}{x-\alpha_{d}}\right]
along its special fibre. Recall Berthelot’s ring
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})
of level-
m
arithmetic differential operators on
\mathscr{X}
for some fixed integer
m\geqslant 0
from
[
13
, p. 46, (2.4.1.4)]
.

Definition 4.4.2
.

For each
\lambda\in K
, define

\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda):=\frac{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}}{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}(x\partial-\lambda)}\in\coh(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}).

We will actually prove the following stronger statement.

Theorem 4.4.3
.

Let
\lambda\in K\backslash\mathbb{Z}
, let
\mathscr{X}
be as above and let
m\geqslant 0
. Then the
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})
-module
\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda)(\mathscr{X})
is simple if
\lambda\in\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{p^{m}-1}i+p^{m}K^{\circ}
, and zero otherwise.

The following elementary statement may help to explain the sets appearing in Theorem
4.4.3
.

Lemma 4.4.4
.

We have
\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\bigcap\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{p^{m}-1}i+p^{m}K^{\circ}
.

Proof.

Certainly the forward inclusion holds. On the other hand, if
\lambda\notin\mathbb{Z}_{p}
then because
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is compact and
K
is Hausdorff, we can find
m\geqslant 0
sufficiently large so that
|\lambda-\alpha|>|p^{m}|
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then
\lambda\notin\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{p^{m}-1}i+p^{m}K^{\circ}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4.1
.

Write
D:=\mathcal{D}^{{\dagger}}_{\varpi}(X)
and
D_{m}:=\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})
for each
m\geqslant 0
. Let
r_{m}:=|p^{m}!|^{\frac{1}{p^{m}}}
; then by Theorem
3.1.9
there is a (not necessarily isometric) isomorphism of
K
-Banach algebras
D_{m}\cong\mathcal{D}_{r_{m}}(X)
. Because the sequence of real numbers
r_{m}
approaches
\varpi
as
m\to\infty
, it follows from Definition
2.3.9
(d) that
D=\colim\limits_{m\geqslant 0}D_{m}
.

Suppose that
\lambda\notin\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then by Lemma
4.4.4
,
\lambda\notin\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{p^{m}-1}i+p^{m}K^{\circ}
for some
m\geqslant 0
, and then
M_{m}:=\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda)(\mathscr{X})
is zero by Theorem
4.4.3
. Therefore
D/D(x\partial-\lambda)=D\otimes_{D_{m}}M_{m}
is zero as well.

Now suppose that
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Since colimits are exact, the
D
-module
M:=D/D(x\partial-\lambda)
is the colimit of the
D_{m}
-modules
M_{m}=D_{m}/D_{m}(x\partial-\lambda)
. Note that the image of the canonical generator
v_{m}
of
M_{m}
inside
M
equals the canonical generator
v
of
M
. Now given any non-zero
w\in M
, because
M=\colim\limits_{m\geqslant 0}M_{m}
we can find
m\geqslant 0
such that
w
is the image in
M
of some non-zero
w_{m}\in M_{m}
. Since
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
M_{m}
is a simple
D_{m}
-module by Theorem
4.4.3
and Lemma
4.4.4
, we can find
Q_{m}\in D
such that
Q_{m}\cdot w_{m}=v_{m}
in
M_{m}
. Hence
Q_{m}\cdot w=v
in
M
. So any non-zero
D
-submodule of
M
contains
v
and is hence equal to
M
. ∎

We will now use Berthelot’s theory of
Frobenius descent
to reduce Theorem
4.4.3
to the case
m=0
. Let
q:=p^{m}
, let
x^{\prime}
be a new local coordinate and let
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
be the formal completion of the punctured affine line
\Spec K^{\circ}\left[x^{\prime},\frac{1}{x^{\prime}-\alpha_{1}^{q}},\cdots,\frac{1}{x^{\prime}-\alpha_{d}^{q}}\right]
along its special fibre. There is a natural lift of the relative Frobenius morphism

F:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{X}^{\prime}

which sends
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
to
x^{q}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
and which is completely determined by this property as a morphism of formal
K^{\circ}
-schemes. In this situation, Berthelot’s Frobenius descent theorem,
[
15
, Théorème 2.3.6]
, tells us that the functors of
Frobenius pullback
and
Frobenius descent

\begin{array}[]{llllll}F^{\ast}&:&\coh(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}})&\longrightarrow&\coh(\mathscr{D}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}),&\mathscr{N}\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}{\otimes}{}\mathscr{N},\quad\mbox{and}\\
F^{\natural}&:&\coh(\mathscr{D}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}})&\longrightarrow&\coh(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}),&\mathscr{M}\mapsto\left(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}^{\vee}\right)\underset{\mathscr{D}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}}{\otimes}{}\mathscr{M}\end{array}

are mutually inverse equivalences of categories, where
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}^{\vee}:=\mathpzc{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}})
. These equivalences extend in a natural way to
p
-adic completions

F^{\natural}:\coh(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\coh(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad F^{\natural}:\coh(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\coh(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}})

which, abusing notation, we will denote by the same symbols. We are now in a position to be able to compute the Frobenius descent of
\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda)
.

Proposition 4.4.5
.

Let
\lambda\in K
and set
\lambda_{i}:=\frac{\lambda-i}{q}
for each
i=0,\ldots,q-1
. Then

F^{\natural}(\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda))\cong\bigoplus_{i=0}^{q-1}\frac{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}}{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}(x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\lambda_{i})}

where
\partial^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
is the unique derivation such that
\partial^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=1
.

Proof.

In
[
15
, Proposition 4.1.2(ii)]
, Berthelot established a canonical isomorphism of
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}
-bimodules

\Phi:F^{\ast}F^{\flat}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}{\otimes}{}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}.

We will use the following explicit description of
\Phi
due to Garnier
[
24
]
; we will follow the clear exposition of Garnier’s results found at
[
4
, §2.2]
. Note that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
-module with basis
\{1,x,\cdots,x^{q-1}\}
; let
\{\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{q-1}\}
be the dual basis for
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}^{\vee}
so that

\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}{\otimes}{}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{i,j=0}^{q-1}x^{i}\otimes\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}\otimes\theta_{j}.

Then the isomorphism
\Phi
is defined as follows:

•

\Phi(x^{i}\otimes Q\otimes\theta_{j})=x^{i}Q^{\circ}Hx^{-j}
for any
Q\in\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
and
0\leqslant i,j<q
, where

•

H:=\frac{1}{q}\sum\limits_{\zeta^{q}=1}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}(\zeta-1)^{k}x^{k}\partial^{[k]}\in\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}x^{q-1}
is the
Dwork operator
, and

•

Q\mapsto Q^{\circ}
is the non-unital ring homomorphism
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}
determined by the rules
1^{\circ}=H,(x^{\prime})^{\circ}=x^{q}H
and
(\partial^{\prime})^{\circ}=(qx^{q-1})^{-1}\partial H
.

Note that
(x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime})^{\circ}=x^{q}(qx^{q-1})^{-1}\partial H=\frac{1}{q}x\partial H
. Therefore

\Phi(x^{i}\otimes(x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\lambda_{i})\otimes\theta_{i})=x^{i}\left(\frac{1}{q}x\partial H-\frac{\lambda-i}{q}H\right)Hx^{-i}=\frac{1}{q}(x\partial-\lambda)x^{i}Hx^{-i}

for any
i=0,\ldots,q-1
, where we used the relations
x^{i}(x\partial)=(x\partial-i)x^{i}
and
H^{2}=H
. Because
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{q-1}x^{i}Hx^{-i}=1
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X}}
by
[
24
, Proposition 2.5.1]
, we see that

(34)

\Phi^{-1}\left(\frac{x\partial-\lambda}{q}\right)=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{q-1}x^{i}\otimes(x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\lambda_{i})\otimes\theta_{i}.

By the Frobenius descent theorem,
[
15
, Théorème 2.3.6]
,

F^{\natural}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}})\quad\underset{\cong}{\overset{F^{\natural}(\Phi^{-1})}{{\longrightarrow}}}\quad F^{\natural}F^{\ast}F^{\flat}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}\quad\cong\quad F^{\flat}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus\limits_{j=0}^{q-1}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}\otimes\theta_{j}

which is a free left
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}
-module of rank
q
. By definition,
\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda)
is the cokernel of right-multiplication by
\frac{x\partial-\lambda}{q}
on
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}
. Using (
34
), we conclude that
F^{\natural}(\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda))
is the cokernel of the ‘diagonal’ left
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}
-linear endomorphism

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{q-1}\cdot(x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\lambda_{i})\otimes 1:\bigoplus\limits_{j=0}^{q-1}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}\otimes\theta_{j}\to\bigoplus\limits_{j=0}^{q-1}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}\otimes\theta_{j}.

The result follows.∎

Corollary 4.4.6
.

Let
\lambda\in K
.

(1)

If
\lambda\notin\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{q-1}i+qK^{\circ}
, then
x\partial-\lambda
is a unit in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}
and
\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda)=0
.

(2)

Otherwise there exists a unique integer
i
such that
0\leqslant i<q
and
\lambda\in i+qK^{\circ}
, and then

F^{\natural}(\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda))\quad\cong\quad\frac{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}}{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}(x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\frac{\lambda-i}{q})}.

Proof.

(a) In this case,
\lambda_{i}=\frac{\lambda-i}{q}\notin K^{\circ}
for all
i=0,\ldots,q-1
, so
|\lambda_{i}|>1
for all
i
. But then
x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\lambda_{i}
is a unit in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}
, so
F^{\natural}(\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda))=0
by Proposition
4.4.5
. So
\mathscr{M}^{(m)}(\lambda)=0
and
x\partial-\lambda
is a unit in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}
because
F^{\natural}
is an equivalence of categories.

(b) Note that if
0\leqslant i<j<q
, then
q
does not divide
j-i
, so
|j-i|>|q|
. Hence
(i+qK^{\circ})\cap(j+qK^{\circ})=\emptyset
, and if
\lambda\in i+qK^{\circ}
then
\lambda\notin j+qK^{\circ}
for any other
j
. This means that
|\lambda_{j}|>1
whenever
j\neq i
, and then
x^{\prime}\partial^{\prime}-\lambda_{j}
is a unit in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}}
as above. Now apply Proposition
4.4.5
.
∎

We now assume that
m=0
and focus on the algebra
D:=\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X}}(\mathscr{X})
, which is
\pi_{K}
-adically complete and separated. It is a lattice inside the Tate-Weyl algebra
D_{K}=\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(0)}_{\mathscr{X},\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{X})
. If
I
is a left ideal in
D
, then we say that
I
is
\pi_{K}
-closed
if the
K^{\circ}
-module
D/I
is torsionfree, or equivalently, if
I=I_{K}\cap D
.

Lemma 4.4.7
.

(1)

The algebra
D
is Noetherian.

(2)

Let
J\leqslant I
be two left ideals of
D
and suppose that
I
is
\pi_{K}
-closed. Then
I+\pi_{K}D=J+\pi_{K}D
implies that
I=J
.

Proof.

(a)
\overline{D}:=D/\pi_{K}D
is the skew-polynomial ring
B[\overline{\partial};\frac{d}{dx}]
where

B:=k\left[x,\frac{1}{x-\overline{\alpha_{1}}},\cdots,\frac{1}{x-\overline{\alpha_{d}}}\right]=\mathcal{O}(\mathscr{X}\times_{K^{\circ}}k)

and
\overline{\alpha_{i}}
denotes the image of
\alpha_{i}
in the residue field
k
of
K^{\circ}
. Since
k
is a field, both
B
and
\overline{D}
are Noetherian rings
1
1

1

This uses the assumption that
K^{\circ}
is a discrete valuation ring.
. The associated graded ring of
D
with respect to the
\pi_{K}
-adic filtration is isomorphic to a polynomial ring
\overline{D}[s]
and is therefore is Noetherian. Hence
D
is Noetherian by
[
33
, Chapter II, §1.2, Proposition 3(1)]
.

(b) Note that
I\cap\pi_{K}D=\pi_{K}I
since
I
is
\pi_{K}
-closed. Hence, by the modular law

I=I\cap(I+\pi_{K}D)=I\cap(J+\pi_{K}D)=J+(I\cap\pi_{K}D)=J+\pi_{K}I

Now
I
is finitely generated by part (a), and
\pi_{K}
lies in the Jacobson radical of
D
because
D
is
\pi_{K}
-adically complete, so
I=J
by Nakayama’s Lemma. ∎

We will assume until the end of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{LocIrred}
that
\lambda\in K^{\circ}
.

Lemma 4.4.8
.

Let
I
be a
\pi_{K}
-closed left ideal of
D
containing
t:=x\partial-\lambda
. Then
I
is stable under the operators
E_{n}:=\binom{\ad(x\partial)}{n}:D_{K}\to D_{K}
for all
n\geqslant 0
.

Proof.

We first consider the case where
I=D
itself. For any
f\in\mathcal{O}(\mathscr{X})
and any
m\geqslant 0
we calculate
\ad(x\partial)(f\partial^{m})=[x\partial,f\partial^{m}]=(x\partial-m)(f)\partial^{m}
. Therefore for any
n\geqslant 0
we see that

(35)

\binom{\ad(x\partial)}{n}(f\partial^{m})=\binom{x\partial-m}{n}(f)\partial^{m}=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n}\binom{-m}{n-i}x^{i}\partial^{[i]}(f)\hskip 2.84544pt\partial^{m}

which implies that
\binom{\ad(x\partial)}{n}
preserves
D
as required. Now,
\ad(x\partial)=\ad(t)
. Letting

\ad(t)_{[n]}:=\ad(t)(\ad(t)-1)\cdots(\ad(t)-n+1)=n!\binom{\ad(t)}{n}

we see that
\ad(t)_{[n]}(D)\subseteq n!D
. Returning to the general case where
I
is not necessarily equal to
D
, note that
\ad(t)(I)=[t,I]\subseteq I
because
t\in I
by assumption. Hence

\ad(t)_{[n]}(I)\subseteq I\cap n!D=n!I

because
D/I
is torsionfree. Dividing through by
n!
we conclude that
I
is stable under
\binom{\ad(t)}{n}=\binom{\ad(x\partial)}{n}
as required.
∎

Proposition 4.4.9
.

Let
I
be a
\pi_{K}
-closed left ideal of
D
properly containing
Dt
. Then for some
n\geqslant 0
, either
x^{n}\in I+\pi_{K}D
or
\partial^{n}\in I+\pi_{K}D
.

Proof.

Let
\overline{I}:=(I+\pi_{K}D)/\pi_{K}D
. Since
I
is
\pi_{K}
-closed, we see that
\overline{I}
strictly contains
\overline{Dt}=(Dt+\pi_{K}D)/\pi_{K}D
by Lemma
4.4.7
(b). Now
\overline{I}
is a left ideal in
\overline{D}=B[\overline{\partial};\frac{d}{dx}]
which is an Ore localisation of the Weyl algebra
W:=k[x][\overline{\partial};\frac{d}{dx}]
at the Ore set consisting of powers of the polynomial
\prod_{i=1}^{d}(x-\overline{\alpha_{i}})
. Therefore, by
[
36
, Proposition 2.1.16(iii)]
,
\overline{I}=\overline{D}\cdot J
where
J:=\overline{I}\cap W
; note that
J
strictly contains
W\overline{t}
because
\overline{I}
strictly contains
\overline{D}\overline{t}
.

Now,
W/W\overline{t}
is a
k
-vector space with basis
\{v_{m}:m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
where
v_{m}:=\overline{x^{m}}+W\overline{t}
if
m\geqslant 0
and
v_{m}:=\overline{\partial^{-m}}+W\overline{t}
if
m<0
. It follows from (
35
) that

E_{n}(v_{m})=\binom{m}{n}v_{m}\quad\mbox{for all}\quad n\geqslant 0,m\in\mathbb{Z}.

Pick a non-zero element
u\in J/W\overline{t}
; then we can write
u
as a finite sum

u=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}u_{m}v_{m}

for some
u_{m}\in k
, not all zero. Suppose that in fact
u_{r}\neq 0
for some
r\in\mathbb{Z}
. The binomial coefficients
\binom{z}{n}
form a basis for the ring of locally constant
k
-valued functions on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We can find such a function,
\epsilon
say, such that
\epsilon(r)=1
and
\epsilon(m)=0
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{r\}
such that
u_{m}\neq 0
. Write
\epsilon=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{N}\epsilon_{n}\binom{z}{n}
; then

\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{N}\epsilon_{n}E_{n}\right)(u)=\sum_{n=0}^{N}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\epsilon_{n}u_{m}\binom{m}{n}v_{m}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}u_{m}v_{m}\epsilon(m)=u_{r}v_{r}.

Now
E_{n}(J)\subseteq J
for all
n\geqslant 0
by Lemma
4.4.8
, so
u_{r}v_{r}\in J/W\overline{t}
. Since
k
is a field, we conclude that
v_{r}\in J/W\overline{t}
and the result follows.
∎

Corollary 4.4.10
.

Let
I
be a
\pi_{K}
-closed left ideal of
D
properly containing
Dt
. Then either

(1)

there exist
a_{n},a_{n-1},\cdots,a_{0}\in K^{\circ}\langle\partial\rangle
with
a_{n}\neq 0
and

a_{n}x^{n}+a_{n-1}x^{n-1}+\cdots+a_{1}x+a_{0}\in I,\quad\mbox{or}\quad

(2)

there exist
a_{n},a_{n-1},\cdots,a_{0}\in\mathcal{O}(\mathscr{X})
with
a_{n}\neq 0
and

a_{n}\partial^{n}+a_{n-1}\partial^{n-1}+\cdots+a_{1}\partial+a_{0}\in I.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.4.9
, we know that either
\overline{x}^{n}\in\overline{I}
or
\overline{\partial}^{n}\in\overline{I}
for some
n\geqslant 0
. Suppose first that
\overline{x}^{n}\in\overline{I}
, and let
S:=\{1,x,\cdots,x^{n-1}\}
. Because the image of
S
in
B/B\overline{x}^{n}
spans it as a
k
-vector space, its image also generates
\overline{D}/\overline{D}\overline{x}^{n}
as a
k[\partial]
-module; hence its image in
\overline{D}/\overline{I}=\overline{D/I}
generates it as a
k[\partial]
-module. Since
D/I
is
\pi_{K}
-adically separated by Lemma
4.4.7
(a) and
[
33
, Chapter II, §1.2, Theorem 10(1)]
, we conclude using
[
33
, Chapter I, Theorem 5.7]
that the image of
S
in
D/I
generates it as a
K^{\circ}\langle\partial\rangle
-module. Hence we may write
x^{n}
as a
K^{\circ}\langle\partial\rangle
-linear combination vectors in this image, and hence (a) holds. Alternatively, if
\overline{\partial}^{n}\in\overline{I}
then the same argument using
T:=\{1,\partial,\cdots,\partial^{n-1}\}
instead of
S
and
\mathcal{O}(\mathscr{X})
instead of
K^{\circ}\langle\partial\rangle
shows that (b) holds.
∎

Proposition 4.4.11
.

Let
I
be a
\pi_{K}
-closed left ideal of
D
properly containing
Dt
and suppose that
\lambda\in K^{\circ}\backslash\mathbb{Z}
. Then either
I\cap K^{\circ}\langle\partial\rangle\neq\{0\}
or
I\cap\mathcal{O}(\mathscr{X})\neq\{0\}
.

Proof.

Let
v_{\lambda}
be the canonical generator of
D/Dt
and let
m\geqslant 1
. Then
