\mathcal{C}_{D^{\infty}(B,K)}
with
\mathcal{C}_{\{0\}/B}
, using
[
9
, Theorem B(c)]
. Recall also the functors
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\{0\}},\ind_{B}^{G},\iota_{+}
and
\iota^{\natural}
from
[
5
]
.

Corollary 7.5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{N}:=\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\{0\}}(\coker\alpha)
. Then there is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/G}

\coker\alpha\cong\ind_{B}^{G}\mathcal{N}.

Proof.

The
G
-orbit of the point
0
is equal to
\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)
, and the stabiliser of
0
under the Möbius action of
G
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)
is equal to
B
. By Proposition
7.5.2
and Lemma
7.5.3
, we see that
\coker\alpha
is an object of
\mathcal{C}^{G\cdot 0}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/G}
. Now use
[
5
, Theorem A]
.
∎

For the following calculations with local cohomology, recall that by
[
48
, §5]
for any rigid analytic space
X
, there is a natural equivalence of categories between the abelian sheaves on
X
and the abelian sheaves on the Huber space
\tilde{X}
associated with
X
. See also
\lx@sectionsign\ref{HuberSpace}
and
[
5
, §2.1]
for further details.

We now begin to study the object
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\{0\}}(\coker\alpha)
in
\mathcal{C}^{\{0\}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/B}
. This sheaf is supported at
\{0\}
only, and therefore is completely determined by its restriction
\mathcal{N}_{|\mathbb{D}}
to
\mathbb{D}
. On the other hand,
\mathcal{N}_{|\mathbb{D}}
lies in
\mathcal{C}^{\{0\}}_{\mathbb{D}/B_{0}}
where
B_{0}:=\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
, and it is therefore completely determined by its global sections
\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})
as a
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{D},B_{0})
-module, in view of
[
9
, Theorem B(c)]
. We first compute the
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{D})
-action on
\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})
as follows.

Lemma 7.5.5
.

\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})
is isomorphic to
H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})
as a
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{D})
-module.

Proof.

Recall the notation
\Upsilon=\mathbb{D}\cap\Omega
. Let
j_{0}:\Upsilon\hookrightarrow\mathbb{D}
be the open inclusion and let
\alpha_{0}
be the restriction of
\alpha
to
\mathbb{D}
. Then we have the short exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{0}}}{{\longrightarrow}}j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon}\to\coker(\alpha_{0})\to 0

of locally Fréchet
\mathcal{D}
-modules on
\mathbb{D}
. By the definition of local cohomology that can be found at
[
27
, p.2]
, we have

\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})=H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},(\coker\alpha)_{|\mathbb{D}})=H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\coker\alpha_{0}).

Now, using
[
27
, Proposition 1.1(b)]
, we get the long exact sequence

(69)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\coker\alpha_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\coker\alpha_{0}).}

Note that this is a long exact sequence of
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{D})
-modules. We claim that the middle terms in this long exact sequence vanish:

(70)

H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})=H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})=0.

By
[
27
, Corollary 1.9]
, these terms appear in the long exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon}).}

The third arrow in the first row is
\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon)\to\mathcal{O}(\Upsilon\backslash\{0\})
, which is an isomorphism since
0\notin\Upsilon
. This already implies that
H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})=0
, and that the second arrow in the second row is injective. The five-term exact sequence of low degree terms associated the Leray spectral sequence applied to the map
j_{0}:\Upsilon\to\mathbb{D}
and the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon}
begins with
0\to H^{1}(\mathbb{D},j_{0,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})\to H^{1}(\Upsilon,\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})
. But
\Upsilon
is a quasi-Stein rigid analytic space, so
H^{1}(\Upsilon,\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon})=0
by Kiehl’s Theorem. This completes the proof of (
70
). The connecting map in (
69
) now gives a
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{D})
-linear isomorphism

\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})=H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\coker\alpha_{0})\cong H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}}).\qed

Lemma 7.5.6
.

We have
\dim_{K}\iota^{\natural}\mathcal{N}=1
.

Proof.

The definition of
\iota^{\natural}
can be found in the statement of
[
5
, Theorem 3.4.17]
:

\iota^{\natural}(\mathcal{M})=\mathpzc{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\{0\}}}\left(\Omega_{\{0\}},\hskip 1.42271pt\iota^{\natural}\hskip 1.42271pt(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})\right)

for any object
\mathcal{M}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\{0\}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/B_{0}}
: first one side-switches
\mathcal{M}
to make a
B_{0}
-equivariant
right

\mathcal{D}
-module on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, then one applies the pullback functor
\iota^{\natural}
for equivariant right
\mathcal{D}
-modules, and then one side-switches back again. The pullback functor for right
\mathcal{D}
-modules
\iota^{\natural}
is defined at
[
5
, Definition 3.4.13]
. We see that only the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module structure on
\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}
is used in the definition of this functor. Since
\mathcal{M}
is supported at
\{0\}
, and since
\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathbb{D})=\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D})dx
is a free
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D})
-module of rank
1
, we see that for any
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\{0\}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/B_{0}}
we have a
K
-vector space isomorphism

\Gamma\left(\mathbb{D},\iota^{\natural}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})\right)\cong\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{D})[x]:=\ker\left(x:\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{D})\right).

Now, applying
[
27
, Corollary 1.9]
again to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}}
, we have the long exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}}).}

Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})=0
, Lemma
7.5.5
now implies that as a
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{D})
-module, we have

\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})\cong H^{1}_{\{0\}}(\mathbb{D},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{D}})\cong\frac{\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\})}{\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D})}.

Now
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D})=K\langle x\rangle
, whereas
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\})
is a subring of
K\langle x,x^{-1}\rangle
. Then we calculate

K\cdot x^{-1}\subseteq\left(\frac{\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\})}{\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D})}\right)[x]\subseteq\left(\frac{K\langle x,x^{-1}\rangle}{K\langle x\rangle}\right)[x]=K\cdot x^{-1}.

So,
\iota^{\natural}\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{D})[x]=\left(\frac{\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D}\backslash\{0\})}{\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{D})}\right)[x]=K\cdot\frac{1}{x}
has
K
-dimension equal to
1
.∎

Let
H
be an open subgroup of
G
; our goal will be now to better understand the restriction of
\coker\alpha
to an object in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/H}
. Using
[
5
, Lemma 2.2.1]
, choose a finite set of representatives
\{s_{1},=1,\cdots,s_{m}\}
for the
H,B
-double cosets in
G
. For each
i=1,\cdots,m
, recall from
[
5
, p.18]
the object

\mathcal{N}_{i}:=\Res^{{}^{s_{i}}B}_{H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B}\left([s_{i}]s_{i,\ast}\mathcal{N}\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/(H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B)}.

Lemma 7.5.7
.

\Res^{G}_{H}\left(\ind_{B}^{G}\mathcal{N}\right)\cong\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{m}\ind_{H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B}^{H}\mathcal{N}_{i}
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/H}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
5
, Lemma 2.3.7]
. ∎

Note that
\mathcal{N}
is supported at
0
. For each
i=1,\cdots,m
, we then see that
\mathcal{N}_{i}
is supported at
x_{i}:=s_{i}\cdot 0\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)
. Therefore

\mathcal{N}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{x_{i}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/(H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\ind_{H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B}^{H}\mathcal{N}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{Hx_{i}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/H}.

Corollary 7.5.8
.

Let
H
be an open subgroup of
G
.

(1)

\Res^{G}_{H}(\coker\alpha)
has length equal to
m=|H\backslash G/B|
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/H}
.

(2)

\Res^{G}_{H}(j_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Omega})
has length
m+1
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/H}
.

Proof.

(a) Fix
i=1,\cdots,m
. Using Corollary
7.5.4
and Lemma
7.5.7
, we see that it is enough to show that
\ind_{H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B}^{H}\mathcal{N}_{i}
is a simple object in
\mathcal{C}^{Hx_{i}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/H}
. Let
\iota_{i}:\{x_{i}\}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the closed embedding; using
[
5
, Corollary 1.3.2]
, it is enough to show that
\iota_{i}^{\natural}\mathcal{N}_{i}
is simple in
\mathcal{C}_{\{x_{i}\}/H\cap{}^{s_{i}}B}
. However, the action of
s_{i}
induces an isomorphism
s_{i,\ast}(\iota^{\natural}\mathcal{N})\cong\iota_{i}^{\natural}\mathcal{N}_{i}
, so Lemma
7.5.6
implies that
\iota_{i}^{\natural}\mathcal{N}_{i}
is a
1
-dimensional
K
-vector space and is therefore simple.

(b) This follows from (a) together with Proposition
7.5.1
(b).
∎

Corollary 7.5.9
.

Let
H
be an open subgroup of
G=\GL_{2}(F)
. Then
\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
is a coadmissible
D(H,K)
-module of length
|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1
.

Proof.

The centre
Z
of
G
acts trivially on
j_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Omega}
. Hence we may view
\Res^{G}_{H}j_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Omega}
as a object in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/\overline{H}}
where
\overline{H}
is the open subgroup
HZ/Z
of
GZ/Z=\mathbb{PGL}_{2}(F)
, and its length is still
|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1
by Corollary
7.5.8
(b). Now apply Theorem
7.4.8
with
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{PGL}_{2}
and the open subgroup
\overline{H}
of
\mathbb{G}(F)
to deduce that
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{1},j_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Omega})=\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
is a coadmissible
D(\overline{H},K)
-module of length
|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1
. The result now follows by inflating back to
D(H,K)
.∎

Recall that
G^{0}=\{g\in\mathbb{GL}_{2}(F):v_{\pi_{F}}(\det g)=0\}
.

Corollary 7.5.10
.

Let
H
be an open subgroup of
G^{0}
and let
\mathscr{L}
be a torsion
G^{0}
-equivariant line bundle with connection on
\Omega
such that
\omega[\mathscr{L}]=0
in
\PicCon(\Omega)
. Then
\mathscr{L}(\Omega)
is a coadmissible
D(H,K)
-module of length
|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1
.

Proof.

By
[
8
, Proposition 3.2.14]
,
\mathscr{L}
is isomorphic as a
G^{0}
-equivariant line bundle with connection to the twist
(\mathcal{O}_{\Omega})_{\chi}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\Omega}
, for some
\chi\in\Hom(G^{0},K^{\times})
. Since
\mathscr{L}
is torsion,
\chi
necessarily has finite order. Since twisting by a character is an equivalence of categories, we deduce from Corollary
7.5.9
that in this case
\mathscr{L}(\Omega)
is a coadmissible
D(H,K)
-module of length
|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1
. ∎

We can finally give a proof of our two main results from the Introduction.

Proof of Theorem
A
.

When
\omega[\mathscr{L}]\neq 0
in
\PicCon(\Omega)
, use Corollary
7.4.9
together with Theorem
7.4.8
. When
\omega[\mathscr{L}]=0
, use Corollary
7.5.10
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
B
.

Applying
[
46
, Proposition 2.3]
we have a decomposition

f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}=\bigoplus_{\psi\in\widehat{\Gamma}}\mathscr{L}_{\psi}

of the
G^{0}
-equivariant vector bundle with flat connection
f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}
into a direct sum of torsion
G^{0}
-equivariant line bundles with connection. Here
\widehat{\Gamma}=\Hom(\Gamma,K^{\times})
is the character group of
\Gamma
. Note that
[\mathscr{L}_{\psi}]\in\PicCon^{G^{0}}(\Omega)_{\tors}
for each
\psi\in\widehat{\Gamma}
. Hence

\mathcal{O}(X)=\bigoplus_{\psi\in\widehat{\Gamma}}\mathscr{L}_{\psi}(\Omega).

By Corollary
7.4.9
, the summand
\mathscr{L}_{\psi}(\Omega)
is a simple coadmissible
D(H,K)
-module whenever
\omega[\mathscr{L}_{\psi}]\neq 0
, and by Corollary
7.5.10
, it is a coadmissible
D(H,K)
-module of length
|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1
when
\omega[\mathscr{L}_{\psi}]=0
. Writing
c_{X}:=\left|\{\psi\in\widehat{\Gamma}:\omega[\mathscr{L}_{\psi}]=0\}\right|
, we deduce that

\ell_{D(H,K)}\left(\mathcal{O}(X)\right)=(|\widehat{\Gamma}|-c_{X})+c_{X}\cdot(|H\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(F)|+1).

It follows from
[
8
, Corollary 3.1.7]
and the proof of
[
47
, Lemma 2.3.4]
that

c_{X}=\dim_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{O}(X_{\mathbf{C}})^{\mathcal{T}(X_{\mathbf{C}})=0}.

But because every connected component of
X_{\mathbf{C}}
is geometrically connected and quasi-Stein, this dimension is just the number of connected components
|\pi_{0}(X_{\mathbf{C}})|
of
X_{\mathbf{C}}
, by
[
8
, Proposition 3.1.6]
. The result follows because
|\widehat{\Gamma}|=|\Gamma|
.
∎
